##### FILE: C:\dse analyzer\backend\app\dse_scraper.py #####
import requests
from bs4 import BeautifulSoup
import pandas as pd
import logging
 
logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
 
DSE_URL = "https://www.dsebd.org/latest_share_price_scroll_l.php"
 
HEADERS = {
    "User-Agent": (
        "Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64) "
        "AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) "
        "Chrome/124.0.0.0 Safari/537.36"
    ),
    "Accept": "text/html,application/xhtml+xml,application/xml;q=0.9,*/*;q=0.8",
    "Accept-Language": "en-US,en;q=0.9",
    "Referer": "https://www.dsebd.org/",
}
 
# DSE table column order (from the actual page):
# #  | Trading Code | LTP | High | Low | Closep | YCP | Change | Trade | Value | Volume
COL_INDEX = {
    "ticker":          1,
    "ltp":             2,   # Last Traded Price
    "high":            3,
    "low":             4,
    "close":           5,   # Today's close
    "ycp":             6,   # Yesterday's closing price
    "change":          7,   # Absolute change
    "trade":           8,   # Number of trades
    "value_mn":        9,   # Value in million BDT
    "volume":         10,   # Volume (shares)
}
 
 
def _safe_float(value: str) -> float | None:
    """
    Convert messy DSE strings to float.
    Handles cases like '2.90-0.10' (merged cells), '1,234.50', '-', '--'.
    """
    if not value or value.strip() in ("-", "--", ""):
        return None
    # Strip commas (thousands separator)
    value = value.replace(",", "").strip()
    # Some cells merge price and change: '25.60-1.20' — take the first number
    if value.count("-") > 0 and not value.startswith("-"):
        value = value.split("-")[0]
    try:
        return float(value)
    except ValueError:
        return None
 
 
def _parse_percent(value: str) -> str | None:
    """Return the percent string as-is (e.g. '-2.50%'), or None if empty."""
    value = value.strip()
    return value if value not in ("", "-", "--") else None
 
 
def fetch_dse_data() -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Scrape latest share prices from DSE and return a clean DataFrame.
 
    Columns returned:
        ticker, ltp, high, low, close, ycp,
        change, percent_change, trade, value_mn, volume
 
    Raises:
        requests.HTTPError  – if DSE returns a non-200 response
        ValueError          – if the expected table is not found in the page
    """
    response = requests.get(DSE_URL, headers=HEADERS, timeout=15)
    response.raise_for_status()
 
    soup = BeautifulSoup(response.text, "html.parser")
 
    # The data lives in <table class="table table-bordered ...">
    # Fall back to any <table> if the class changes.
    table = (
        soup.find("table", {"class": lambda c: c and "table" in c})
        or soup.find("table")
    )
    if table is None:
        raise ValueError("Could not find the stock data table on the DSE page.")
 
    stocks = []
    rows = table.find_all("tr")
 
    for row in rows[1:]:   # skip header row
        cols = row.find_all("td")
        if len(cols) < 10:
            continue
 
        def cell(idx: int) -> str:
            return cols[idx].get_text(strip=True).replace("\xa0", "")
 
        ticker = cell(COL_INDEX["ticker"])
        if not ticker:
            continue
 
        # Percent change is usually the last column (index 11) or second-to-last
        # We compute it ourselves to be safe
        ltp    = _safe_float(cell(COL_INDEX["ltp"]))
        ycp    = _safe_float(cell(COL_INDEX["ycp"]))
        change = _safe_float(cell(COL_INDEX["change"]))
 
        percent_change: float | None = None
        if change is not None and ycp and ycp != 0:
            percent_change = round(change / ycp * 100, 2)
 
        stocks.append({
            "ticker":         ticker,
            "ltp":            ltp,
            "high":           _safe_float(cell(COL_INDEX["high"])),
            "low":            _safe_float(cell(COL_INDEX["low"])),
            "close":          _safe_float(cell(COL_INDEX["close"])),
            "ycp":            ycp,
            "change":         change,
            "percent_change": percent_change,
            "trade":          _safe_float(cell(COL_INDEX["trade"])),
            "value_mn":       _safe_float(cell(COL_INDEX["value_mn"])),
            "volume":         _safe_float(cell(COL_INDEX["volume"])),
        })
 
    if not stocks:
        raise ValueError(
            "Scraped 0 stocks — the page structure may have changed. "
            "Check COL_INDEX in dse_scraper.py against the live page."
        )
 
    df = pd.DataFrame(stocks)
    logger.info("Fetched %d stocks from DSE", len(df))
    return df


##### FILE: C:\dse analyzer\backend\app\fundamental_quality.py #####
"""
app/analysis/fundamental_quality.py
───────────────────────────────────
Add-on factors for fundamental.py that catch "structurally cheap" traps the
ratio-only scorer misses — e.g. Padma/Jamuna Oil, where low P/E, sub-book P/B,
fat dividend and low (interest-bearing) debt all fire bullish, but the cheapness
is justified by interest-income-driven earnings, working-capital-float cash, and
state ownership.

Each function returns a FundamentalSignal (same dataclass as fundamental.py) so
they slot straight into the existing scoring loop.

TIER 1 — implementable today, NO new data (uses fields already in Fundamentals):
    _score_ownership          → govt_holding, public_holding, sponsor_holding
    _score_liquidity          → daily turnover (ltp*volume/1e6) from Stock
    _score_earnings_stability → eps_2021..eps_2025

TIER 2 — needs ~3 new scraped fields (see "SCRAPER / MODEL ADDITIONS" at bottom):
    _score_earnings_quality   → revenue_mn        (net-income-to-revenue check)
    _score_real_net_cash      → cash_mn, total_liabilities_mn

Plus compute_score_with_buckets(), which replaces the raw bull/bear sum in
fundamental.py so the three valuation signals can't triple-count cheapness.
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import statistics
from typing import Optional

# Reuse the existing dataclass so signals are interchangeable
from .fundamental import FundamentalSignal


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# TIER 1 — no new data required
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def _score_ownership(
    govt_holding:    Optional[float],
    public_holding:  Optional[float],
    sponsor_holding: Optional[float] = None,
) -> Optional[FundamentalSignal]:
    """
    State control + thin float carry a PERMANENT discount the market applies on
    purpose. The old scorer is blind to this and reads the resulting low P/E as
    'undervalued'. Holdings are percentages (0-100).
    """
    if govt_holding is None and public_holding is None:
        return None

    # State-controlled: capital allocation serves the government, not minorities
    if govt_holding is not None and govt_holding >= 30:
        return FundamentalSignal(
            "Ownership", "Bearish",
            f"Govt holds {govt_holding:.0f}% — state-controlled; persistent "
            f"discount & minority-unfriendly capital allocation", weight=2.0)

    # Thin free float → illiquid, hard to exit, value can stay trapped for years
    if public_holding is not None and public_holding < 15:
        return FundamentalSignal(
            "Ownership", "Bearish",
            f"Public float only {public_holding:.0f}% — thin float, "
            f"illiquidity discount", weight=1.5)

    if public_holding is not None and public_holding >= 35:
        return FundamentalSignal(
            "Ownership", "Bullish",
            f"Healthy public float {public_holding:.0f}%", weight=0.5)

    return FundamentalSignal("Ownership", "Neutral",
                             "Ownership structure unremarkable", weight=0.0)


def _score_liquidity(turnover_mn: Optional[float]) -> Optional[FundamentalSignal]:
    """
    turnover_mn = daily traded value = ltp * volume / 1e6  (pass from Stock row).
    Illiquidity is itself a reason a cheap stock stays cheap — and a real risk
    for anyone who has to exit.
    """
    if turnover_mn is None:
        return None
    if turnover_mn < 3:
        return FundamentalSignal(
            "Liquidity", "Bearish",
            f"Turnover ~{turnover_mn:.0f}mn — illiquid; exit risk, value can "
            f"stay trapped", weight=1.5)
    if turnover_mn < 10:
        return FundamentalSignal("Liquidity", "Neutral",
            f"Turnover ~{turnover_mn:.0f}mn — thin", weight=0.0)
    return FundamentalSignal("Liquidity", "Bullish",
        f"Turnover ~{turnover_mn:.0f}mn — liquid", weight=0.5)


def _score_earnings_stability(
    eps_years: list[Optional[float]]
) -> Optional[FundamentalSignal]:
    """
    Complements the existing _score_eps_trend (which only measures DIRECTION).
    This measures VOLATILITY + one-off spikes. A rate-driven jump (Padma's +38%)
    shows up as a recent spike vs the multi-year median → flag, don't extrapolate.
    Pass [eps_2021, eps_2022, eps_2023, eps_2024, eps_2025].
    """
    vals = [e for e in eps_years if e is not None]
    if len(vals) < 3:
        return None
    mean = statistics.mean(vals)
    if mean <= 0:
        return None
    cv     = statistics.pstdev(vals) / mean        # coefficient of variation
    median = statistics.median(vals)
    spike  = vals[-1] > median * 1.5               # latest year is an outlier high

    if cv > 0.6 or spike:
        why = f"CV {cv:.0%}" + (" + recent spike" if spike else "")
        return FundamentalSignal(
            "Earnings Stability", "Bearish",
            f"EPS erratic ({why}) — low-quality/cyclical earnings, "
            f"don't extrapolate the latest year", weight=1.5)
    if cv < 0.25:
        return FundamentalSignal(
            "Earnings Stability", "Bullish",
            f"EPS steady (CV {cv:.0%}) — durable earnings", weight=1.0)
    return FundamentalSignal("Earnings Stability", "Neutral",
        f"EPS moderately variable (CV {cv:.0%})", weight=0.0)


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# TIER 2 — needs new scraped fields (revenue_mn, cash_mn, total_liabilities_mn)
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def _score_earnings_quality(
    eps:                Optional[float],
    shares_outstanding: Optional[float],
    revenue_mn:         Optional[float],
) -> Optional[FundamentalSignal]:
    """
    THE key Padma/Jamuna factor. If net income is a large fraction of (or exceeds)
    revenue, the profit is NOT coming from the core business — it's interest/other
    income on a cash pile. A fuel distributor cannot earn 60%+ net margin on
    operations; that ratio is a giant red flag for earnings quality + rate risk.
    """
    if eps is None or not shares_outstanding or not revenue_mn or revenue_mn <= 0:
        return None
    net_income_mn = eps * shares_outstanding / 1_000_000.0
    ratio = net_income_mn / revenue_mn
    if ratio > 0.6:
        return FundamentalSignal(
            "Earnings Quality", "Bearish",
            f"Net income is {ratio:.0%} of revenue — profit is largely "
            f"non-operating (interest/other), rate-sensitive, not core business",
            weight=2.5)
    if ratio > 0.3:
        return FundamentalSignal(
            "Earnings Quality", "Neutral",
            f"Net income {ratio:.0%} of revenue — verify income mix", weight=0.0)
    return FundamentalSignal(
        "Earnings Quality", "Bullish",
        f"Net income {ratio:.0%} of revenue — operationally driven", weight=1.0)


def _score_real_net_cash(
    cash_mn:               Optional[float],
    total_liabilities_mn:  Optional[float],
    market_cap_mn:         Optional[float],
) -> Optional[FundamentalSignal]:
    """
    'Trapped cash' detector. A naive net-cash screen sees a huge cash balance and
    a near-zero interest-bearing debt and concludes 'trades below net cash'. But if
    that cash is offset by trade payables / dues to the parent (BPC), the true net
    cash after ALL liabilities is small. Catches the negative-EV illusion.
    Requires total_liabilities_mn (NOT just interest-bearing total_debt_mn).
    """
    if cash_mn is None or total_liabilities_mn is None or not market_cap_mn:
        return None
    true_net_cash = cash_mn - total_liabilities_mn
    looks_cash_rich = cash_mn > market_cap_mn * 0.5
    if looks_cash_rich and true_net_cash < market_cap_mn * 0.10:
        return FundamentalSignal(
            "Balance Sheet Quality", "Bearish",
            "Large cash is offset by liabilities (working-capital float) — "
            "'trading below net cash' is an accounting illusion", weight=2.0)
    if true_net_cash > market_cap_mn * 0.30:
        return FundamentalSignal(
            "Balance Sheet Quality", "Bullish",
            "Genuine net cash after ALL liabilities — real balance-sheet strength",
            weight=1.5)
    return None


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# Valuation de-duplication — replaces the raw bull/bear sum in fundamental.py
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

# These three are really one underlying fact: "cheap vs accounting figures".
# Counting them separately gives a cash-rich value trap up to 7.5 bullish weight.
VALUATION_FACTORS = {"P/E Ratio", "P/B Ratio", "NAV Safety Margin"}
VALUATION_CAP = 3.5     # max combined contribution from the whole valuation bucket


def compute_score_with_buckets(signals: list[FundamentalSignal]) -> float:
    """
    Drop-in replacement for the bull/bear/maxi block in compute_fundamental_analysis.
    Identical maths, except the valuation signals are collapsed into ONE capped
    contribution so cheapness is counted once, not three times.
    """
    bull = bear = maxi = 0.0
    val_bull = val_bear = 0.0

    for s in signals:
        if s.value == "Neutral":
            continue
        if s.name in VALUATION_FACTORS:
            if s.value == "Bullish":
                val_bull += s.weight
            else:
                val_bear += s.weight
            continue
        if s.value == "Bullish":
            bull += s.weight
        else:
            bear += s.weight
        maxi += s.weight

    # Collapse the valuation bucket to a single net contribution, capped at ±CAP
    val_net = max(-VALUATION_CAP, min(VALUATION_CAP, val_bull - val_bear))
    if val_net >= 0:
        bull += val_net
    else:
        bear += -val_net
    if val_bull or val_bear:
        maxi += VALUATION_CAP   # bucket can contribute at most ±CAP to the range

    if maxi <= 0:
        return 50.0
    raw = (bull - bear) / maxi          # -1 .. +1
    return round(50 + raw * 50, 1)      # 0 .. 100


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# INTEGRATION — wiring into fundamental.py
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
#
# 1) In compute_fundamental_analysis(), accept the new inputs:
#        govt_holding=None, public_holding=None, sponsor_holding=None,
#        turnover_mn=None, eps_years=None,
#        revenue_mn=None, cash_mn=None, total_liabilities_mn=None,
#        market_cap_mn=None,
#    (crud.py already loads the Fundamentals row — pass these straight through;
#     turnover_mn = stock.ltp * stock.volume / 1e6 from the Stock row.)
#
# 2) After the existing signals.append(...) calls, add:
#        from .fundamental_quality import (
#            _score_ownership, _score_liquidity, _score_earnings_stability,
#            _score_earnings_quality, _score_real_net_cash,
#            compute_score_with_buckets)
#        for sig in (
#            _score_ownership(govt_holding, public_holding, sponsor_holding),
#            _score_liquidity(turnover_mn),
#            _score_earnings_stability(eps_years or []),
#            _score_earnings_quality(eps, shares_outstanding, revenue_mn),
#            _score_real_net_cash(cash_mn, total_liabilities_mn, market_cap_mn),
#        ):
#            if sig:
#                signals.append(sig)
#
# 3) Replace the bull/bear/maxi/score block with:
#        score = compute_score_with_buckets(signals)
#    (keep all the existing caps/penalties below it unchanged.)
#
# 4) RE-CALIBRATE the BUY/HOLD cutoffs. Adding bearish-capable factors will pull
#    some scores down; re-check the 62/42 thresholds against a sample so you don't
#    silently turn old BUYs into HOLDs. Spot-check PADMAOIL/JAMUNAOIL drop to HOLD
#    and a genuine name (e.g. IDLC) stays BUY.
#
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# SCRAPER / MODEL ADDITIONS (Tier 2 only)
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
#
# The ownership + debt fields already come from your DSE company-page scraper
# (dse_scraper.py → models.Fundamentals). The DSE financial-highlights page that
# already yields short_term_debt_mn / govt_holding also carries cash, total
# liabilities and turnover/revenue. To enable Tier 2:
#
#   a) models.py → add to Fundamentals:
#          revenue_mn            = Column(Float, nullable=True)
#          cash_mn               = Column(Float, nullable=True)
#          total_liabilities_mn  = Column(Float, nullable=True)
#
#   b) dse_scraper.py → parse those three rows from the same financial-highlights
#      table you already read debt/ownership from, and write them on upsert.
#
#   c) Run your migration path (init_db.py / migrate) to add the columns.
#
# Until (a)-(c) are done, the Tier-2 scorers simply return None (missing data) and
# the Tier-1 factors alone already flag Padma/Jamuna via ownership + erratic EPS.


##### FILE: C:\dse analyzer\backend\app\fundamental_scraper.py #####
"""
app/fundamental_scraper.py
──────────────────────────
Fetches EPS, P/E, and sector for all DSE stocks from amarstock.com.

Endpoint:  GET https://www.amarstock.com/market-pe
Response:  { "scripPEDataList": [ { "Scrip": "ACI", "Eps": -7.4, "PE": -33.3, ... } ] }

Confirmed field names from live data (2026-05-24):
    Scrip             — DSE trading code
    Eps               — Reported EPS (annual, BDT) — USE THIS
    AnnualizedEPS     — Annualized estimate — less reliable
    Q1Eps..Q4Eps      — Quarterly EPS breakdown
    PE                — P/E ratio (negative when loss-making)
    WeightedPE        — Market-cap weighted PE
    BusinessSegment   — Sector name
    LTP               — Last traded price
    MarketCapital     — Market cap in BDT
    TotalSecurities   — Shares outstanding

NAV is not available on this endpoint — left as None.
"""

import requests
import pandas as pd
import logging
import time

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

API_URL = "https://www.amarstock.com/market-pe"

HEADERS = {
    "User-Agent": (
        "Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64) "
        "AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) "
        "Chrome/124.0.0.0 Safari/537.36"
    ),
    "Accept":           "application/json, text/javascript, */*; q=0.01",
    "Accept-Language":  "en-US,en;q=0.9",
    "X-Requested-With": "XMLHttpRequest",
    "Referer":          "https://www.amarstock.com/market-pe-ratio",
}


def _safe_float(value) -> float | None:
    if value is None:
        return None
    try:
        v = float(str(value).replace(",", "").strip())
        # Return None for zero PE/EPS (means no data, not actually zero)
        return v if v == v else None   # filter NaN
    except (ValueError, TypeError):
        return None


def _zero_to_none(value) -> float | None:
    """Convert 0 to None — amarstock uses 0 as a missing value placeholder."""
    v = _safe_float(value)
    if v == 0.0:
        return None
    return v


def fetch_pe_data(timeout: int = 60, retries: int = 3) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Fetch EPS, P/E, sector for all DSE stocks from amarstock.

    Returns DataFrame with columns:
        ticker, eps, pe, nav, sector, company,
        q1_eps, q2_eps, q3_eps, q4_eps,
        market_cap, shares_outstanding, ltp_from_amarstock

    Raises ConnectionError if all retries fail.
    """
    last_error = None

    for attempt in range(1, retries + 1):
        try:
            logger.info(
                "Fetching fundamentals from amarstock (attempt %d/%d)...",
                attempt, retries
            )

            resp = requests.get(API_URL, headers=HEADERS, timeout=timeout)
            resp.raise_for_status()

            data  = resp.json()
            items = data.get("scripPEDataList") or []

            if not items:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"scripPEDataList missing or empty. "
                    f"Response keys: {list(data.keys())}"
                )

            rows = []
            for item in items:
                ticker = str(item.get("Scrip") or "").strip().upper()
                if not ticker:
                    continue

                # Eps is annual reported EPS
                eps = _safe_float(item.get("Eps"))

                # Raw PE from AmarStock
                pe = _zero_to_none(item.get("PE"))

                # AmarStock LTP
                ltp = _zero_to_none(item.get("LTP"))

                # Fallback: derive PE if missing
                if pe is None and eps is not None and eps > 0 and ltp is not None:
                    pe = round(ltp / eps, 2)

                rows.append({
                    "ticker":              ticker,
                    "eps":                 eps,
                    "pe":                  pe,
                    "nav":                 None,   # not available on this endpoint
                    "sector":              str(item.get("BusinessSegment") or "").strip() or None,
                    "company":             None,   # not in this response
                    "q1_eps":              _safe_float(item.get("Q1Eps")),
                    "q2_eps":              _safe_float(item.get("Q2Eps")),
                    "q3_eps":              _safe_float(item.get("Q3Eps")),
                    "q4_eps":              _safe_float(item.get("Q4Eps")),
                    "annualized_eps":      _safe_float(item.get("AnnualizedEPS")),
                    "market_cap":          _zero_to_none(item.get("MarketCapital")),
                    "shares_outstanding":  _zero_to_none(item.get("TotalSecurities")),
                    "ltp_amarstock":       ltp,
                })

            df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
            logger.info(
                "Fetched %d stocks. %d have EPS, %d have PE.",
                len(df),
                df["eps"].notna().sum(),
                df["pe"].notna().sum(),
            )
            return df

        except requests.Timeout as e:
            last_error = e
            logger.warning(
                "Attempt %d/%d timed out. %s",
                attempt, retries,
                "Retrying in 5s..." if attempt < retries else "Giving up."
            )
            if attempt < retries:
                time.sleep(5)

        except ValueError as e:
            raise   # don't retry parsing errors

        except Exception as e:
            last_error = e
            logger.warning("Attempt %d/%d failed: %s", attempt, retries, e)
            if attempt < retries:
                time.sleep(3)

    raise ConnectionError(
        f"Failed to fetch fundamentals after {retries} attempts. "
        f"Last error: {last_error}"
    )


##### FILE: C:\dse analyzer\backend\app\history_loader.py #####
"""
app/history_loader.py
─────────────────────
Backfills historical OHLCV data for all DSE stocks using bdshare.

Usage:
    POST /load-history?days=365          — backfill 1 year for all stocks
    POST /load-history?days=365&ticker=ACI — single stock only

bdshare handles retries, rate limiting, and DSE scraping internally.
We just loop through tickers, fetch, and upsert into price_history.
"""

import pandas as pd
import logging
import time
from datetime import date, timedelta, datetime
from typing import Optional

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


def _safe_float(value) -> Optional[float]:
    if value is None:
        return None
    try:
        v = float(str(value).replace(",", "").strip())
        return None if v != v else v   # filter NaN
    except (ValueError, TypeError):
        return None


def fetch_historical(ticker: str, start: date, end: date) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Fetch historical daily OHLCV for one ticker using bdshare.
    Returns empty DataFrame on failure.
    """
    try:
        from bdshare import get_historical_data
        df = get_historical_data(
            start.strftime("%Y-%m-%d"),
            end.strftime("%Y-%m-%d"),
            ticker.upper(),
        )
        return df if df is not None and not df.empty else pd.DataFrame()
    except Exception as e:
        logger.debug("bdshare failed for %s: %s", ticker, e)
        return pd.DataFrame()


def load_history_for_ticker(
    ticker: str,
    start: date,
    end: date,
    db,
    existing_dates: Optional[set] = None,
) -> dict:
    """
    Fetch and upsert price history for a single ticker.
    Returns {"inserted": int, "updated": int, "skipped": int}
    """
    from . import models

    counts = {"inserted": 0, "updated": 0, "skipped": 0}

    df = fetch_historical(ticker, start, end)
    if df.empty:
        counts["skipped"] += 1
        return counts

    # Load existing dates for this ticker if not provided
    if existing_dates is None:
        existing_rows = (
            db.query(models.PriceHistory.date)
            .filter(models.PriceHistory.ticker == ticker.upper())
            .all()
        )
        existing_dates = {r.date for r in existing_rows}

    for row_date, row in df.iterrows():
        # row_date is the index (a date or datetime)
        if hasattr(row_date, "date"):
            row_date = row_date.date()
        elif isinstance(row_date, str):
            try:
                row_date = datetime.strptime(row_date, "%Y-%m-%d").date()
            except ValueError:
                continue

        close  = _safe_float(row.get("close"))
        ltp    = _safe_float(row.get("ltp"))
        canon  = close or ltp
        if canon is None:
            continue

        fields = {
            "open":           _safe_float(row.get("open")),
            "high":           _safe_float(row.get("high")),
            "low":            _safe_float(row.get("low")),
            "close":          canon,
            "ltp":            ltp,
            "volume":         _safe_float(row.get("volume")),
            "value_mn":       _safe_float(row.get("value")),
            "trade":          _safe_float(row.get("trade")),
            "change":         _safe_float(row.get("change") if "change" in row.index else None),
            "percent_change": None,
        }

        # Compute percent_change from ycp if available
        ycp = _safe_float(row.get("ycp"))
        if ycp and ycp != 0 and fields["close"]:
            fields["percent_change"] = round(
                (fields["close"] - ycp) / ycp * 100, 2
            )

        if row_date in existing_dates:
            # Update existing row
            hist_row = (
                db.query(models.PriceHistory)
                .filter(
                    models.PriceHistory.ticker == ticker.upper(),
                    models.PriceHistory.date   == row_date,
                )
                .first()
            )
            if hist_row:
                for field, val in fields.items():
                    if val is not None:
                        setattr(hist_row, field, val)
                counts["updated"] += 1
        else:
            hist_row = models.PriceHistory(
                ticker = ticker.upper(),
                date   = row_date,
                **fields,
            )
            db.add(hist_row)
            existing_dates.add(row_date)
            counts["inserted"] += 1

    db.commit()
    return counts


def bulk_load_history(
    tickers: list[str],
    days: int = 365,
    db = None,
    pause: float = 0.5,
) -> dict:
    """
    Backfill history for a list of tickers.

    Args:
        tickers:  list of DSE trading codes
        days:     how many calendar days back to fetch
        db:       SQLAlchemy session
        pause:    seconds between requests (bdshare has built-in rate limiting
                  but an extra pause avoids hammering DSE)

    Returns summary dict.
    """
    end   = date.today()
    start = end - timedelta(days=days)

    total   = len(tickers)
    summary = {"total_tickers": total, "inserted": 0, "updated": 0, "skipped": 0, "failed": 0}

    logger.info(
        "Backfilling %d days of history for %d tickers (%s → %s)...",
        days, total, start, end,
    )

    for i, ticker in enumerate(tickers, 1):
        try:
            counts = load_history_for_ticker(ticker, start, end, db)
            summary["inserted"] += counts["inserted"]
            summary["updated"]  += counts["updated"]
            summary["skipped"]  += counts["skipped"]

            if i % 20 == 0 or i == total:
                logger.info(
                    "  Progress: %d/%d | inserted=%d updated=%d failed=%d",
                    i, total,
                    summary["inserted"],
                    summary["updated"],
                    summary["failed"],
                )
        except Exception as e:
            logger.warning("Failed for %s: %s", ticker, e)
            summary["failed"] += 1

        if i < total:
            time.sleep(pause)

    return summary

from fastapi.staticfiles import StaticFiles
from datetime import date, timedelta
from typing import Optional
from fastapi import FastAPI, Depends, HTTPException, Query
from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

from .database import SessionLocal, engine
from . import models, schemas
from .crud import (
    create_stock, get_stock, get_all_stocks, delete_stock,
    save_dse_data, get_price_history, get_price_history_range,
    get_fundamentals, save_fundamentals,
)
from .dse_scraper import fetch_dse_data
from .fundamental_scraper import fetch_pe_data
from .analysis.technical import compute_technical_analysis
from .analysis.fundamental import compute_fundamental_analysis

models.Base.metadata.create_all(bind=engine)

app = FastAPI(
    title="DSE Analyzer API",
    description="Fundamental + technical analysis for Dhaka Stock Exchange",
    version="3.0.0",
)

app.mount("/static", StaticFiles(directory="."), name="static")
from fastapi.middleware.cors import CORSMiddleware

app.add_middleware(
    CORSMiddleware,
    allow_origins=["*"],
    allow_methods=["*"],
    allow_headers=["*"],
)

# ── DB dependency ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def get_db():
    db = SessionLocal()
    try:
        yield db
    finally:
        db.close()

    return {"message": "DSE Analyzer API", "version": "3.0.0"}


# ── Data loading ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

@app.post("/load-dse", response_model=schemas.LoadDSEResponse, tags=["data"])
def load_dse(db: Session = Depends(get_db)):
    """
    Scrape latest prices from DSE and upsert stocks + price_history.
    Call this daily (or set up a scheduler).
    """
    try:
        df = fetch_dse_data()
    except Exception as e:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=502, detail=f"DSE price scrape failed: {e}")

    summary = save_dse_data(df, db)
    return {"message": "DSE data loaded", "total_scraped": len(df), **summary}


@app.post("/load-fundamentals", response_model=schemas.LoadFundamentalsResponse, tags=["data"])
def load_fundamentals(db: Session = Depends(get_db)):
    """
    Fetch EPS, P/E, NAV for all DSE stocks from amarstock's market-pe API.
    Single request covers all ~400 stocks instantly.
    Call this daily — values update when companies publish quarterly results.
    """
    try:
        df = fetch_pe_data()
    except Exception as e:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=502, detail=f"Fundamentals scrape failed: {e}")

    summary = save_fundamentals(df, db)
    return {
        "message": "Fundamentals loaded",
        "total_scraped": len(df),
        **summary,
    }

@app.get("/stocks/{ticker}/history", response_model=schemas.HistoryResponse, tags=["history"])
def price_history(
    ticker: str,
    limit:  int = Query(200, ge=1, le=1000),
    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
):
    stock = get_stock(db, ticker)
    if not stock:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"'{ticker.upper()}' not found")
    rows = get_price_history(db, ticker, limit=limit)
    return {"ticker": ticker.upper(), "company": stock.company, "count": len(rows), "history": rows}


@app.get("/stocks/{ticker}/history/range", response_model=schemas.HistoryResponse, tags=["history"])
def price_history_range(
    ticker: str,
    start:  date = Query(...),
    end:    date = Query(...),
    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
):
    if start > end:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="start must be before end")
    stock = get_stock(db, ticker)
    if not stock:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"'{ticker.upper()}' not found")
    rows = get_price_history_range(db, ticker, start, end)
    return {"ticker": ticker.upper(), "company": stock.company, "count": len(rows), "history": rows}

@app.get(
    "/stocks/{ticker}/analysis/technical",
    response_model=schemas.TechnicalAnalysisOut,
    tags=["analysis"],
)
def technical_analysis(
    ticker: str,
    days:   int = Query(200, ge=14, le=1000),
    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
):
    """RSI, MACD, SMA, EMA, Bollinger Bands with BUY/HOLD/SELL signal."""
    stock = get_stock(db, ticker)
    if not stock:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"'{ticker.upper()}' not found")
    rows   = get_price_history(db, ticker, limit=days)
    result = compute_technical_analysis(ticker, rows)
    return {
        "ticker":         result.ticker,
        "current_price":  result.current_price,
        "recommendation": result.recommendation,
        "score":          result.score,
        "data_days":      result.data_days,
        "warning":        result.warning,
        "indicators":     vars(result.indicators),
        "signals":        [vars(s) for s in result.signals],
    }


@app.get(
    "/stocks/{ticker}/analysis/fundamental",
    response_model=schemas.FundamentalAnalysisOut,
    tags=["analysis"],
)
def fundamental_analysis(ticker: str, db: Session = Depends(get_db)):
    """P/E, P/B, EPS, NAV, dividend yield scoring with BUY/HOLD/SELL signal."""
    stock = get_stock(db, ticker)
    if not stock:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"'{ticker.upper()}' not found")

    fund = get_fundamentals(db, ticker)

    eps_hist = {}
    if fund:
        for yr, attr in [(2021,"eps_2021"),(2022,"eps_2022"),(2023,"eps_2023"),(2024,"eps_2024"),(2025,"eps_2025")]:
            v = getattr(fund, attr, None)
            if v is not None: eps_hist[yr] = v

    result = compute_fundamental_analysis(
        ticker        = ticker,
        current_price = stock.ltp or stock.close or 0,
        eps           = fund.eps      if fund else None,
        pe            = fund.pe       if fund else None,
        nav           = fund.nav      if fund else None,
        dividend      = fund.dividend if fund else None,
        roe           = fund.roe      if fund else None,
        paid_up       = fund.paid_up_capital if fund else None,
        sector        = stock.sector,
        eps_history   = eps_hist if eps_hist else None,
        total_debt_mn = getattr(fund, "total_debt_mn", None) if fund else None,
        shares_outstanding = getattr(fund, "shares_outstanding", None) if fund else None,
    )

    return {
        "ticker":         result.ticker,
        "current_price":  result.current_price,
        "recommendation": result.recommendation,
        "score":          result.score,
        "eps":            result.eps,
        "pe":             result.pe,
        "nav":            result.nav,
        "pb":             result.pb,
        "dividend_yield": result.dividend_yield,
        "roe":            result.roe,
        "sector":         result.sector,
        "sector_pe":      result.sector_pe,
        "warning":        result.warning,
        "signals":        [vars(s) for s in result.signals],
    }


@app.get(
    "/stocks/{ticker}/analysis",
    response_model=schemas.CombinedAnalysisOut,
    tags=["analysis"],
)
def combined_analysis(
    ticker:       str,
    days:         int   = Query(200, ge=14, le=1000),
    tech_weight:  float = Query(0.45, ge=0.0, le=1.0, description="Technical weight (0-1)"),
    fund_weight:  float = Query(0.55, ge=0.0, le=1.0, description="Fundamental weight (0-1)"),
    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
):
    """
    Full combined analysis — technical + fundamental in one response.

    Weights default to 55% fundamental / 45% technical.
    Adjust via query params: ?tech_weight=0.5&fund_weight=0.5
    """
    stock = get_stock(db, ticker)
    if not stock:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"'{ticker.upper()}' not found")

    # Normalise weights so they always sum to 1
    total = tech_weight + fund_weight
    tw    = tech_weight / total
    fw    = fund_weight / total

    # Technical
    history_rows = get_price_history(db, ticker, limit=days)
    tech = compute_technical_analysis(ticker, history_rows)

    # Fundamental
    fund_row = get_fundamentals(db, ticker)
    eps_hist2 = {}
    if fund_row:
        for yr, attr in [(2021,"eps_2021"),(2022,"eps_2022"),(2023,"eps_2023"),(2024,"eps_2024"),(2025,"eps_2025")]:
            v = getattr(fund_row, attr, None)
            if v is not None: eps_hist2[yr] = v
    fund = compute_fundamental_analysis(
        ticker        = ticker,
        current_price = stock.ltp or stock.close or 0,
        eps           = fund_row.eps      if fund_row else None,
        pe            = fund_row.pe       if fund_row else None,
        nav           = fund_row.nav      if fund_row else None,
        dividend      = fund_row.dividend if fund_row else None,
        roe           = fund_row.roe      if fund_row else None,
        paid_up       = fund_row.paid_up_capital if fund_row else None,
        sector        = stock.sector,
        eps_history   = eps_hist2 if eps_hist2 else None,
        total_debt_mn = getattr(fund_row, "total_debt_mn", None) if fund_row else None,
        shares_outstanding = getattr(fund_row, "shares_outstanding", None) if fund_row else None,
    )

    # Combined score
    combined_score = round(tech.score * tw + fund.score * fw, 1)

    if combined_score >= 62:
        combined_rec = "BUY"
    elif combined_score >= 42:
        combined_rec = "HOLD"
    else:
        combined_rec = "SELL"

    # Merge warnings
    warnings = [w for w in [tech.warning, fund.warning] if w]
    warning  = " | ".join(warnings) if warnings else None

    return {
        "ticker":         ticker.upper(),
        "current_price":  stock.ltp or stock.close or 0,
        "sector":         stock.sector,
        "company":        stock.company,
        "recommendation": combined_rec,
        "combined_score": combined_score,
        "tech_score":     tech.score,
        "fund_score":     fund.score,
        "data_days":      tech.data_days,
        "warning":        warning,
        "technical": {
            "ticker":         tech.ticker,
            "current_price":  tech.current_price,
            "recommendation": tech.recommendation,
            "score":          tech.score,
            "data_days":      tech.data_days,
            "warning":        tech.warning,
            "indicators":     vars(tech.indicators),
            "signals":        [vars(s) for s in tech.signals],
        },
        "fundamental": {
            "ticker":         fund.ticker,
            "current_price":  fund.current_price,
            "recommendation": fund.recommendation,
            "score":          fund.score,
            "eps":            fund.eps,
            "pe":             fund.pe,
            "nav":            fund.nav,
            "pb":             fund.pb,
            "dividend_yield": fund.dividend_yield,
            "roe":            fund.roe,
            "sector":         fund.sector,
            "sector_pe":      fund.sector_pe,
            "warning":        fund.warning,
            "signals":        [vars(s) for s in fund.signals],
        },
    }


# ── Screener ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

from .analysis.screener import run_screener

@app.get("/screener", response_model=schemas.ScreenerResponse, tags=["analysis"])
def screener(
    sector:          Optional[str]   = Query(None, description="e.g. 'Pharmaceuticals and Chemicals' or 'Bank'"),
    min_eps:         Optional[float] = Query(None, description="Minimum EPS"),
    max_eps:         Optional[float] = Query(None, description="Maximum EPS"),
    min_pe:          Optional[float] = Query(None, description="Minimum P/E"),
    max_pe:          Optional[float] = Query(None, description="Maximum P/E (e.g. 15 for value stocks)"),
    profitable_only: bool            = Query(False, description="Only include stocks with positive EPS"),
    min_tech_score:  Optional[float] = Query(None, description="Minimum technical score 0-100"),
    min_score:       Optional[float] = Query(None, description="Minimum combined score 0-100"),
    recommendation:  Optional[str]   = Query(None, description="BUY | HOLD | SELL"),
    limit:           int             = Query(30, ge=1, le=200),
    sort_by:         str             = Query("combined_score", description="combined_score | tech_score | fund_score | pe | eps"),
    sort_dir:        str             = Query("desc", description="asc | desc"),
    tech_weight:     float           = Query(0.45, ge=0.0, le=1.0),
    fund_weight:     float           = Query(0.55, ge=0.0, le=1.0),
    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
):
    """
    Screen all DSE stocks by fundamental and technical criteria.

    Examples:
      /screener?profitable_only=true&max_pe=15&recommendation=BUY
      /screener?sector=Bank&min_tech_score=60&limit=10
      /screener?min_score=65&sort_by=pe&sort_dir=asc
    """
    from typing import Optional as Opt
    results = run_screener(
        db              = db,
        sector          = sector,
        min_eps         = min_eps,
        max_eps         = max_eps,
        min_pe          = min_pe,
        max_pe          = max_pe,
        profitable_only = profitable_only,
        min_tech_score  = min_tech_score,
        min_score       = min_score,
        recommendation  = recommendation,
        limit           = limit,
        sort_by         = sort_by,
        sort_dir        = sort_dir,
        tech_weight     = tech_weight,
        fund_weight     = fund_weight,
    )

    active_filters = {k: v for k, v in {
        "sector": sector, "min_eps": min_eps, "max_eps": max_eps,
        "min_pe": min_pe, "max_pe": max_pe, "profitable_only": profitable_only,
        "min_tech_score": min_tech_score, "min_score": min_score,
        "recommendation": recommendation,
    }.items() if v is not None and v is not False}

    return {
        "total":   len(results),
        "filters": active_filters,
        "results": results,
    }


# ── Historical data loader ────────────────────────────────────────────────────

from .history_loader import bulk_load_history

@app.post("/load-history", tags=["data"])
def load_history(
    days:   int          = Query(365, ge=30, le=1500, description="Days of history to backfill"),
    ticker: Optional[str] = Query(None, description="Single ticker — omit for all stocks"),
    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
):
    """
    Backfill historical OHLCV price data using bdshare.

    - Single stock:  POST /load-history?days=365&ticker=ACI  (~2 seconds)
    - All stocks:    POST /load-history?days=365             (~15-30 minutes)

    Run single-stock first to verify, then run all stocks overnight.
    Data is upserted — safe to re-run, won't duplicate rows.
    """
    if ticker:
        tickers = [ticker.upper()]
    else:
        tickers = [s.ticker for s in get_all_stocks(db)]

    if not tickers:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="No stocks in DB. Call POST /load-dse first.")

    try:
        summary = bulk_load_history(tickers=tickers, days=days, db=db)
    except Exception as e:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=502, detail=f"History load failed: {e}")

    return {
        "message": "History loaded",
        "date_range": f"{(date.today() - __import__('datetime').timedelta(days=days)).isoformat()} → {date.today().isoformat()}",
        **summary,
    }
from fastapi.responses import FileResponse

@app.get('/dashboard')
def dashboard():
    return FileResponse('dashboard.html', media_type='text/html; charset=utf-8')




from app.models import Portfolio as PortfolioModel, Stock as StockModel

@app.get('/portfolio')
def get_portfolio(db: Session = Depends(get_db)):
    holdings = db.query(PortfolioModel).all()
    result = []
    for h in holdings:
        stock = db.query(StockModel).filter(StockModel.ticker == h.ticker).first()
        if not stock:
            continue
        current_price = stock.ltp or 0
        invested = h.buy_price * h.quantity
        current_value = current_price * h.quantity
        pl = current_value - invested
        pl_pct = (pl / invested * 100) if invested > 0 else 0
        rec, score = 'HOLD', 0
        try:
            history_rows = get_price_history(db, h.ticker, limit=200)
            tech = compute_technical_analysis(h.ticker, history_rows)
            fund_row = get_fundamentals(db, h.ticker)
            eps_hist = {}
            if fund_row:
                for yr, attr in [(2021,"eps_2021"),(2022,"eps_2022"),(2023,"eps_2023"),(2024,"eps_2024"),(2025,"eps_2025")]:
                    v = getattr(fund_row, attr, None)
                    if v is not None: eps_hist[yr] = v
            fund = compute_fundamental_analysis(
                ticker=h.ticker, current_price=current_price,
                eps=fund_row.eps if fund_row else None,
                pe=fund_row.pe if fund_row else None,
                nav=fund_row.nav if fund_row else None,
                sector=stock.sector,
                eps_history=eps_hist if eps_hist else None,
                total_debt_mn=getattr(fund_row, "total_debt_mn", None) if fund_row else None,
                shares_outstanding=getattr(fund_row, "shares_outstanding", None) if fund_row else None,
            )
            combined = round(tech.score * 0.45 + fund.score * 0.55, 1)
            if combined >= 62: rec = "BUY"
            elif combined >= 42: rec = "HOLD"
            else: rec = "SELL"
            score = combined
        except Exception:
            pass
        result.append({'ticker': h.ticker, 'buy_price': h.buy_price, 'quantity': h.quantity,
            'current_price': current_price, 'invested': round(invested, 2),
            'current_value': round(current_value, 2), 'pl': round(pl, 2),
            'pl_pct': round(pl_pct, 2), 'recommendation': rec, 'score': score,
            'sector': stock.sector})
    return result

@app.post('/portfolio')
def add_holding(ticker: str, buy_price: float, quantity: float, db: Session = Depends(get_db)):
    ticker = ticker.upper()
    stock = db.query(StockModel).filter(StockModel.ticker == ticker).first()
    if not stock:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f'Ticker {ticker} not found')
    existing = db.query(PortfolioModel).filter(PortfolioModel.ticker == ticker).first()
    if existing:
        existing.buy_price = buy_price
        existing.quantity = quantity
    else:
        db.add(PortfolioModel(ticker=ticker, buy_price=buy_price, quantity=quantity))
    db.commit()
    return {'message': f'{ticker} added to portfolio'}

@app.delete('/portfolio/{ticker}')
def remove_holding(ticker: str, db: Session = Depends(get_db)):
    h = db.query(PortfolioModel).filter(PortfolioModel.ticker == ticker.upper()).first()
    if not h:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail='Not found')
    db.delete(h)
    db.commit()
    return {'message': f'{ticker} removed'}

@app.get('/portfolio/analysis')
def portfolio_analysis(db: Session = Depends(get_db)):
    holdings = get_portfolio(db)
    if not holdings:
        return {'message': 'Portfolio is empty'}
    total_invested = sum(h['invested'] for h in holdings)
    total_value = sum(h['current_value'] for h in holdings)
    total_pl = total_value - total_invested
    total_pl_pct = (total_pl / total_invested * 100) if total_invested > 0 else 0
    avg_score = sum(h['score'] for h in holdings) / len(holdings)
    sell = [h for h in holdings if h['recommendation'] == 'SELL']
    hold = [h for h in holdings if h['recommendation'] == 'HOLD']
    buy_more = [h for h in holdings if h['recommendation'] == 'BUY']
    try:
        from .analysis.screener import run_screener
        screener_results = run_screener(db=db, profitable_only=True, max_pe=15.0, limit=5)
        top_buys = [vars(r) for r in screener_results] if screener_results else []
    except Exception:
        top_buys = []
    return {'summary': {'total_invested': round(total_invested, 2), 'total_value': round(total_value, 2),
        'total_pl': round(total_pl, 2), 'total_pl_pct': round(total_pl_pct, 2),
        'avg_score': round(avg_score, 1), 'holdings_count': len(holdings)},
        'sell_suggestions': sell, 'hold': hold, 'buy_more': buy_more, 'top_buy_opportunities': top_buys}

from sqlalchemy import (
    Column, Integer, String, Float,
    DateTime, Date, UniqueConstraint, ForeignKey, Index
)
from sqlalchemy.orm import relationship
from sqlalchemy.sql import func
from .database import Base


class Stock(Base):
    __tablename__ = "stocks"

    id      = Column(Integer, primary_key=True, index=True)

    # Identity
    ticker  = Column(String, unique=True, index=True, nullable=False)
    company = Column(String, nullable=True)
    sector  = Column(String, nullable=True)

    # Legacy column — kept so old DBs don't break before migration.
    # New code uses `ltp`. migrate_db.py copies price → ltp.
    price   = Column(Float, nullable=True)

    # Latest snapshot (overwritten on every /load-dse call)
    ltp             = Column(Float, nullable=True)   # Last Traded Price
    high            = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    low             = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    close           = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    ycp             = Column(Float, nullable=True)   # Yesterday's closing price
    change          = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    percent_change  = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    trade           = Column(Float, nullable=True)   # Number of trades
    value_mn        = Column(Float, nullable=True)   # Traded value (million BDT)
    volume          = Column(Float, nullable=True)

    # Housekeeping
    updated_at = Column(DateTime(timezone=True), onupdate=func.now())
    created_at = Column(DateTime(timezone=True), server_default=func.now())

    # Relationship — lets you do stock.history to get all PriceHistory rows
    history = relationship(
        "PriceHistory",
        back_populates="stock",
        order_by="PriceHistory.date",
        cascade="all, delete-orphan",
    )

    def __repr__(self):
        return f"<Stock ticker={self.ticker} ltp={self.ltp}>"


class PriceHistory(Base):
    """
    One row per (ticker, date).

    Populated automatically on every /load-dse call.
    Use `close` as the canonical daily price for technical indicators
    (RSI, MACD, SMA, etc.) — it is the official end-of-day price.
    `ltp` is also stored because intraday calls may land before the
    official close is published, giving you the last trade at that moment.

    UniqueConstraint on (ticker, date) means:
      - First scrape of the day  → INSERT
      - Any later scrape same day → UPDATE (upsert)
    so you can call /load-dse as often as you like without duplicates.
    """
    __tablename__ = "price_history"

    id      = Column(Integer, primary_key=True, index=True)
    ticker  = Column(
        String,
        ForeignKey("stocks.ticker", ondelete="CASCADE"),
        nullable=False,
        index=True,
    )
    date    = Column(Date, nullable=False)   # calendar date only, no time component

    # OHLCV — mirrors the DSE scraper columns
    open    = Column(Float, nullable=True)   # DSE doesn't publish open; always None for now
    high    = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    low     = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    close   = Column(Float, nullable=True)   # official close — use this for indicators
    ltp     = Column(Float, nullable=True)   # last traded price at time of scrape
    volume  = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    value_mn = Column(Float, nullable=True)  # traded value in million BDT
    trade   = Column(Float, nullable=True)   # number of trades
    change  = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    percent_change = Column(Float, nullable=True)

    scraped_at = Column(DateTime(timezone=True), server_default=func.now())

    # Relationship back to Stock
    stock = relationship("Stock", back_populates="history")

    # (ticker, date) must be unique — enforces one row per stock per day
    __table_args__ = (
        UniqueConstraint("ticker", "date", name="uq_price_history_ticker_date"),
        Index("ix_price_history_ticker_date", "ticker", "date"),
    )

    def __repr__(self):
        return f"<PriceHistory ticker={self.ticker} date={self.date} close={self.close}>"


class Fundamentals(Base):
    """
    One row per ticker — updated on every POST /load-fundamentals call.
    Source: amarstock.com/market-pe (single API call for all ~400 stocks)
    """
    __tablename__ = "fundamentals"

    id      = Column(Integer, primary_key=True, index=True)
    ticker  = Column(
        String,
        ForeignKey("stocks.ticker", ondelete="CASCADE"),
        unique=True,
        nullable=False,
        index=True,
    )

    # Core fundamentals
    eps              = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    pe               = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    nav              = Column(Float, nullable=True)

    # Quarterly EPS
    q1_eps           = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    q2_eps           = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    q3_eps           = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    q4_eps           = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    annualized_eps   = Column(Float, nullable=True)

    # Market data
    market_cap          = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    shares_outstanding  = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    ltp_amarstock       = Column(Float, nullable=True)

    # Manually enriched
    paid_up_capital  = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    dividend         = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    roe              = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    eps_2021         = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    eps_2022         = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    eps_2023         = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    eps_2024         = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    eps_2025         = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    nav_2021         = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    nav_2022         = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    nav_2023         = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    nav_2024         = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    nav_2025         = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    eps_trend        = Column(Float, nullable=True)

    # DSE scraped data
    nav_year         = Column(String, nullable=True)
    eps_dse          = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    short_term_debt_mn = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    long_term_debt_mn  = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    total_debt_mn    = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    sponsor_holding  = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    govt_holding     = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    institute_holding = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    foreign_holding  = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    public_holding   = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    debt_equity      = Column(Float, nullable=True)
    pb_ratio         = Column(Float, nullable=True)

    # Housekeeping
    updated_at = Column(DateTime(timezone=True), onupdate=func.now())
    created_at = Column(DateTime(timezone=True), server_default=func.now())

    def __repr__(self):
        return f"<Fundamentals ticker={self.ticker} eps={self.eps} pe={self.pe}>"


class Portfolio(Base):
    __tablename__ = "portfolio"
    id = Column(Integer, primary_key=True, index=True)
    ticker = Column(String, unique=True, nullable=False)
    buy_price = Column(Float, nullable=False)
    quantity = Column(Float, nullable=False)
    added_at = Column(String, default="")



##### FILE: C:\dse analyzer\backend\app\position_sizer.py #####
"""
position_sizer.py — risk-based position sizing for U1254
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Replaces the "no name > 15%" *notional* cap with proper fixed-fractional
*risk* sizing, then layers the notional, liquidity, and cluster caps on top.

Core idea (what a desk actually does):
    risk budget  = equity * risk_pct           (e.g. 1% of equity per trade)
    shares       = risk_budget / (entry - stop) (so every trade risks the SAME taka)
    then cap by:  notional (<=15% of book), liquidity (<=X% of ADV)

This fixes the flaw that a tight-stop and a wide-stop name at the same 15%
weight carry wildly different ACTUAL risk. Here, risk is constant; the
position SIZE flexes with the stop distance.

Run `python position_sizer.py` for a worked demo on the U1254 book.
"""

from __future__ import annotations
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Optional

# DSE all-in round-trip friction: commission + laga/howla + 0.05% sell tax + slippage.
# Tune to your LankaBangla schedule; 1.2% is a realistic default for liquid names,
# higher for thin ones (spread dominates).
DSE_ROUNDTRIP_COST = 0.012


@dataclass
class SizeResult:
    ticker: str
    shares: int
    capital: float
    pct_of_book: float
    risk_taka: float
    risk_pct_actual: float
    bound_by: str                       # "risk" | "notional" | "liquidity"
    rr_to_target: Optional[float] = None
    net_target_pct: Optional[float] = None
    warnings: list = field(default_factory=list)

    def line(self) -> str:
        rr = f"{self.rr_to_target:.2f}" if self.rr_to_target else "—"
        return (f"{self.ticker:11} {self.shares:5d} sh  "
                f"৳{self.capital:>8,.0f}  {self.pct_of_book:>4.1f}% book  "
                f"risk ৳{self.risk_taka:>5,.0f} ({self.risk_pct_actual:.2f}%)  "
                f"R:R {rr:>4}  [{self.bound_by}]")


def size_position(ticker: str, equity: float, entry: float, stop: float,
                  target: Optional[float] = None,
                  risk_pct: float = 0.01,
                  notional_cap_pct: float = 0.15,
                  adv_mn: Optional[float] = None,
                  max_adv_participation: float = 0.10,
                  roundtrip_cost: float = DSE_ROUNDTRIP_COST) -> SizeResult:
    """
    equity         : total account equity (PV + cash)
    entry, stop    : prices; stop MUST be below entry for a long
    target         : optional, to compute reward:risk and net-of-cost target
    risk_pct       : fraction of equity to risk if the stop is hit (default 1%)
    notional_cap_pct : hard cap on position as % of book (default 15%)
    adv_mn         : average daily traded VALUE in millions (liquidity) — optional
    max_adv_participation : don't let the position exceed this % of one day's ADV
    """
    if entry <= stop:
        raise ValueError(f"{ticker}: entry ({entry}) must be above stop ({stop}) for a long.")

    warnings: list[str] = []
    risk_per_share = entry - stop

    # 1) RISK-based count — the primary driver
    shares_risk = (equity * risk_pct) / risk_per_share
    # 2) NOTIONAL cap
    shares_notional = (equity * notional_cap_pct) / entry
    # 3) LIQUIDITY cap — never be more than X% of a day's traded value
    shares_liq = float("inf")
    if adv_mn:
        shares_liq = (adv_mn * 1e6 * max_adv_participation) / entry

    candidates = [(shares_risk, "risk"),
                  (shares_notional, "notional"),
                  (shares_liq, "liquidity")]
    shares_float, bound_by = min(candidates, key=lambda x: x[0])
    shares = int(shares_float)

    capital = shares * entry
    risk_taka = shares * risk_per_share

    rr = net_target = None
    if target:
        rr = (target - entry) / risk_per_share
        net_target = round((target - entry) / entry - roundtrip_cost, 4)
        if rr < 1.5:
            warnings.append(f"R:R {rr:.2f} (<1.5) — thin reward for the risk; pass or tighten target.")
        if net_target is not None and net_target <= 0:
            warnings.append("Target does NOT clear round-trip costs — this trade loses money even if it works.")

    if bound_by == "notional":
        warnings.append("Capped by 15% notional (stop is tight, so risk-sizing wanted more). Fine.")
    if bound_by == "liquidity":
        warnings.append(f"Capped by liquidity (>{max_adv_participation:.0%} of ADV). Exit may be slow — size down further or skip.")
    if risk_per_share / entry > 0.10:
        warnings.append(f"Stop is {risk_per_share/entry:.0%} away — very wide; risk-sizing gives only {shares} sh.")
    if shares == 0:
        warnings.append("Risk/liquidity caps round to ZERO shares — trade too small to take or stop too wide.")

    return SizeResult(ticker, shares, round(capital), round(capital / equity * 100, 1),
                      round(risk_taka), round(risk_taka / equity * 100, 3),
                      bound_by, round(rr, 2) if rr else None, net_target, warnings)


# ── Cluster / correlation cap ────────────────────────────────────────────────
# Treat correlated names as ONE risk unit. The book learned this reactively
# (both banks soft at once); make it a rule.
CLUSTER_MAP = {
    "Bank": "FINANCIALS", "NBFI": "FINANCIALS", "Financial Institutions": "FINANCIALS",
    "Insurance": "INSURANCE",
    "Pharma": "PHARMA", "Pharmaceuticals": "PHARMA",
    "Textile": "TEXTILE",
    "Fuel/Power": "FUEL", "Fuel and Power": "FUEL",
    "Cement": "MATERIALS", "Engineering": "MATERIALS",
}

def check_cluster_caps(positions: list[tuple[str, float, str]], equity: float,
                       cluster_cap_pct: float = 0.30,
                       single_sector_cap_pct: float = 0.20) -> list[str]:
    """
    positions: list of (ticker, capital_taka, sector).
    Flags any correlated cluster over cluster_cap_pct and any single sector
    over single_sector_cap_pct. Returns a list of human-readable flags.
    """
    sector_tot: dict[str, float] = {}
    cluster_tot: dict[str, float] = {}
    for _t, cap, sector in positions:
        sector_tot[sector] = sector_tot.get(sector, 0) + cap
        cl = CLUSTER_MAP.get(sector, sector)
        cluster_tot[cl] = cluster_tot.get(cl, 0) + cap

    flags = []
    for sec, cap in sorted(sector_tot.items(), key=lambda x: -x[1]):
        pct = cap / equity
        if pct > single_sector_cap_pct:
            flags.append(f"SECTOR {sec}: {pct:.0%} of book (> {single_sector_cap_pct:.0%} cap)")
    for cl, cap in sorted(cluster_tot.items(), key=lambda x: -x[1]):
        pct = cap / equity
        if pct > cluster_cap_pct:
            flags.append(f"CLUSTER {cl}: {pct:.0%} of book (> {cluster_cap_pct:.0%} cap) — correlated names, treat as one risk")
    return flags


# ── Demo on the U1254 book ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
if __name__ == "__main__":
    EQUITY = 193_300   # PV + cash from the 14-Jun statement

    print("=" * 78)
    print(f"RISK-BASED SIZING DEMO  ·  equity ৳{EQUITY:,}  ·  1% risk per trade")
    print("=" * 78)
    # (ticker, entry, stop, target, adv_mn)
    plays = [
        ("PRAGATIINS", 76.90, 71.80, 82.20, 60),
        ("IDLC",       41.00, 38.40, 44.50, 24),
        ("DOREENPWR",  31.60, 30.20, 34.30, 31),
        ("BXPHARMA",  125.70,122.00,136.00, 132),   # tight stop → notional-capped
    ]
    for tk, e, s, t, adv in plays:
        r = size_position(tk, EQUITY, e, s, t, risk_pct=0.01, adv_mn=adv)
        print(r.line())
        for w in r.warnings:
            print(f"            ⚠ {w}")

    print("\n" + "-" * 78)
    print("Contrast: what 'equal-weight ~7,900 each' actually risked vs 1% sizing")
    print("-" * 78)
    for tk, e, s, t, adv in plays[:3]:
        eq_shares = int(7900 // e)
        eq_risk = eq_shares * (e - s)
        print(f"{tk:11} equal-wt {eq_shares:4d} sh → risk ৳{eq_risk:,.0f} "
              f"({eq_risk/EQUITY*100:.2f}% of book)  vs 1% target ৳{EQUITY*0.01:,.0f}")

    print("\n" + "-" * 78)
    print("CLUSTER CHECK on the current 14-Jun book")
    print("-" * 78)
    book = [
        ("BXPHARMA",29923,"Pharma"),("SQURPHARMA",25884,"Pharma"),("ACMELAB",20094,"Pharma"),
        ("CITYBANK",14700,"Bank"),("PRIMEBANK",10325,"Bank"),
        ("IDLC",9864,"NBFI"),("DBH",9045,"NBFI"),
        ("MALEKSPIN",10010,"Textile"),("ENVOYTEX",6552,"Textile"),("PTL",8714,"Textile"),
        ("PRAGATIINS",11460,"Insurance"),("CONFIDCEM",8854,"Cement"),
        ("BSC",8618,"Miscellaneous"),("MPETROLEUM",4182,"Fuel/Power"),
    ]
    for f in check_cluster_caps(book, EQUITY):
        print("  ⚠", f)

import requests
import logging

logging.basicConfig(
    filename=r"C:\dse analyzer\backend\auto_update.log",
    level=logging.INFO,
    format="%(asctime)s %(levelname)s %(message)s"
)

BASE = "http://127.0.0.1:8000"

def post(path):
    try:
        r = requests.post(f"{BASE}{path}", timeout=1800)
        logging.info(f"{path} -> {r.status_code} {r.text[:200]}")
    except Exception as e:
        logging.error(f"{path} failed: {e}")

logging.info("=== Auto update started ===")
post("/load-dse")
post("/load-fundamentals")
post("/load-history?days=30")
logging.info("=== Auto update complete ===")


##### FILE: C:\dse analyzer\backend\backtest.py #####
"""
backtest.py — walk-forward backtest for the U1254 swing ruleset
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Answers the question a desk asks BEFORE trusting a mechanical system:
    "Does this ruleset actually have an edge — and how big?"

It reuses the project's OWN indicator math (technical.py: Wilder RSI,
EMA-seeded MACD, SMA) so the test reflects the real signals, not a
re-implementation. It walks each ticker's PriceHistory day by day,
recomputing indicators point-in-time (no look-ahead), enters when the
entry filter passes, and exits on stop / target / time-stop — net of
DSE transaction costs.

Outputs: trade count, win rate, avg win/loss, EXPECTANCY (the number that
matters), profit factor, max drawdown, avg hold. Plus a parameter sweep so
you can test whether RSI 50–68 actually beats other bands instead of
trusting it on faith.

USAGE (from the backend dir, where dse.db and technical.py live):
    python backtest.py                      # full backtest, default params
    python backtest.py --sweep rsi          # sweep the RSI entry band
    python backtest.py --tickers BXPHARMA,IDLC,SQURPHARMA

Self-test (no DB needed):
    python backtest.py --selftest
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import sqlite3, argparse, statistics, sys
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Optional

# Reuse the project's EXACT indicator functions. Run from the dir containing technical.py.
try:
    from app.analysis.technical import _sma, _rsi, _macd_series
except ImportError:
    # allow running the self-test standalone
    _sma = _rsi = _macd_series = None

DSE_ROUNDTRIP_COST = 0.012   # commission + 0.05% tax + slippage, round trip


# ── Strategy parameters (everything tunable — that's the point) ───────────────
@dataclass
class Params:
    rsi_min: float = 50.0
    rsi_max: float = 68.0
    require_macd_pos: bool = True
    require_above_sma20: bool = True
    require_above_sma50: bool = True
    vol_ratio_min: float = 1.3
    pct52_min: float = -22.0          # room-to-run window
    pct52_max: float = -2.0
    stop_atr_mult: float = None        # if set, stop = entry - mult*ATR; else use SMA20/pct
    stop_pct: float = 0.045            # fallback fixed stop distance
    target_R: float = 1.9              # target = entry + target_R * risk  (R-multiple)
    time_stop_days: int = 7
    warmup: int = 55                   # need >=50 for SMA50 + buffer
    cost: float = DSE_ROUNDTRIP_COST


@dataclass
class Trade:
    ticker: str
    entry_i: int
    entry_px: float
    exit_px: float
    bars_held: int
    exit_reason: str        # stop | target | time
    gross_ret: float
    net_ret: float          # after round-trip cost


# ── Indicator snapshot at a point in time (no look-ahead) ────────────────────
def _snapshot(closes, highs, lows, vols, i):
    """Compute the indicators the entry filter needs, using data UP TO day i only."""
    c = closes[: i + 1]
    if len(c) < 51:
        return None
    sma20 = _sma(c, 20)
    sma50 = _sma(c, 50)
    rsi = _rsi(c, 14)
    _macd, _sig, hist = _macd_series(c)
    avg_vol = sum(vols[max(0, i - 19): i + 1]) / min(20, i + 1) if vols else None
    vol_ratio = (vols[i] / avg_vol) if (avg_vol and avg_vol > 0) else None
    w52h = max(highs[max(0, i - 251): i + 1]) if highs else None
    pct52 = ((c[-1] - w52h) / w52h * 100) if w52h else None
    return dict(price=c[-1], sma20=sma20, sma50=sma50, rsi=rsi,
                macd_hist=hist, vol_ratio=vol_ratio, pct52=pct52)


def _entry_ok(s, p: Params) -> bool:
    if s is None or None in (s["sma20"], s["sma50"], s["rsi"], s["macd_hist"]):
        return False
    if not (p.rsi_min <= s["rsi"] <= p.rsi_max):                return False
    if p.require_macd_pos and s["macd_hist"] <= 0:             return False
    if p.require_above_sma20 and s["price"] <= s["sma20"]:     return False
    if p.require_above_sma50 and s["price"] <= s["sma50"]:     return False
    if p.vol_ratio_min and (s["vol_ratio"] or 0) < p.vol_ratio_min: return False
    if s["pct52"] is None or not (p.pct52_min <= s["pct52"] <= p.pct52_max): return False
    return True


def simulate_ticker(ticker, closes, highs, lows, vols, p: Params) -> list[Trade]:
    trades: list[Trade] = []
    i = p.warmup
    n = len(closes)
    while i < n - 1:
        s = _snapshot(closes, highs, lows, vols, i)
        if not _entry_ok(s, p):
            i += 1
            continue
        # Enter at NEXT bar's open ~ next close (no same-bar look-ahead)
        entry_i = i + 1
        entry_px = closes[entry_i]
        risk = entry_px * p.stop_pct
        stop_px = entry_px - risk
        target_px = entry_px + p.target_R * risk

        exit_px = exit_reason = None
        j = entry_i + 1
        while j < n:
            lo, hi = lows[j], highs[j]
            if lo <= stop_px:                       # stop first (conservative)
                exit_px, exit_reason = stop_px, "stop"; break
            if hi >= target_px:
                exit_px, exit_reason = target_px, "target"; break
            if (j - entry_i) >= p.time_stop_days:
                exit_px, exit_reason = closes[j], "time"; break
            j += 1
        if exit_px is None:                          # ran out of data
            exit_px, exit_reason, j = closes[-1], "eod", n - 1

        gross = (exit_px - entry_px) / entry_px
        net = gross - p.cost
        trades.append(Trade(ticker, entry_i, entry_px, exit_px, j - entry_i,
                            exit_reason, round(gross, 4), round(net, 4)))
        i = j + 1                                    # no overlapping trades per ticker
    return trades


# ── Aggregate metrics ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def summarize(trades: list[Trade]) -> dict:
    if not trades:
        return {"trades": 0}
    rets = [t.net_ret for t in trades]
    wins = [r for r in rets if r > 0]
    losses = [r for r in rets if r <= 0]
    gross_win = sum(wins)
    gross_loss = -sum(losses)
    # max drawdown on the sequential equity curve (1 unit compounded per trade)
    eq, peak, mdd = 1.0, 1.0, 0.0
    for r in rets:
        eq *= (1 + r)
        peak = max(peak, eq)
        mdd = min(mdd, eq / peak - 1)
    expectancy = statistics.mean(rets)
    return {
        "trades": len(trades),
        "win_rate": len(wins) / len(trades),
        "avg_win": statistics.mean(wins) if wins else 0.0,
        "avg_loss": statistics.mean(losses) if losses else 0.0,
        "expectancy": expectancy,                      # avg net return per trade
        "expectancy_per_100": expectancy * 100,
        "profit_factor": (gross_win / gross_loss) if gross_loss else float("inf"),
        "max_drawdown": mdd,
        "avg_bars": statistics.mean(t.bars_held for t in trades),
        "total_compounded": eq - 1,
        "exit_mix": {r: sum(1 for t in trades if t.exit_reason == r)
                     for r in ("stop", "target", "time", "eod")},
    }


def print_summary(title, m):
    print(f"\n── {title} ──")
    if m["trades"] == 0:
        print("  no trades triggered"); return
    print(f"  trades {m['trades']}  ·  win {m['win_rate']:.0%}  ·  "
          f"avg win {m['avg_win']*100:+.1f}%  avg loss {m['avg_loss']*100:+.1f}%")
    print(f"  EXPECTANCY {m['expectancy_per_100']:+.2f}% / trade  ·  "
          f"profit factor {m['profit_factor']:.2f}  ·  max DD {m['max_drawdown']*100:.1f}%")
    print(f"  avg hold {m['avg_bars']:.1f} bars  ·  exits {m['exit_mix']}")
    verdict = ("EDGE looks real" if m["expectancy"] > 0.003 and m["profit_factor"] > 1.3
               else "NO reliable edge — do not size up" if m["expectancy"] <= 0
               else "marginal — costs eat most of it")
    print(f"  → {verdict}")


# ── DB loading ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
def load_ticker(con, ticker):
    q = ("SELECT date, COALESCE(close, ltp), high, low, volume "
         "FROM price_history WHERE ticker=? ORDER BY date ASC")
    rows = con.execute(q, (ticker,)).fetchall()
    closes = [float(r[1]) for r in rows if r[1] is not None]
    highs = [float(r[2]) if r[2] is not None else float(r[1]) for r in rows if r[1] is not None]
    lows = [float(r[3]) if r[3] is not None else float(r[1]) for r in rows if r[1] is not None]
    vols = [float(r[4]) if r[4] is not None else 0.0 for r in rows if r[1] is not None]
    return closes, highs, lows, vols


def run(db="dse.db", tickers=None, p: Params = None):
    p = p or Params()
    con = sqlite3.connect(db)
    if not tickers:
        tickers = [r[0] for r in con.execute(
            "SELECT DISTINCT ticker FROM price_history").fetchall()]
    all_trades = []
    for tk in tickers:
        closes, highs, lows, vols = load_ticker(con, tk)
        if len(closes) < p.warmup + 5:
            continue
        all_trades += simulate_ticker(tk, closes, highs, lows, vols, p)
    con.close()
    return all_trades


# ── Parameter sweep (kills the "RSI 50–68 is arbitrary" objection) ───────────
def sweep_rsi(db, tickers=None):
    print("\n=== RSI ENTRY-BAND SWEEP ===")
    for lo, hi in [(45, 65), (50, 68), (50, 72), (55, 70), (40, 60), (55, 75)]:
        p = Params(rsi_min=lo, rsi_max=hi)
        m = summarize(run(db, tickers, p))
        if m["trades"]:
            print(f"  RSI {lo}-{hi}:  n={m['trades']:4d}  win {m['win_rate']:.0%}  "
                  f"expectancy {m['expectancy_per_100']:+.2f}%  PF {m['profit_factor']:.2f}  "
                  f"maxDD {m['max_drawdown']*100:.0f}%")
        else:
            print(f"  RSI {lo}-{hi}:  no trades")


# ── Synthetic self-test (proves the engine works without the DB) ─────────────
def selftest():
    import math, random
    random.seed(7)
    # Build a trending series with noise so SOME entries trigger and resolve
    closes = []
    px = 100.0
    for t in range(400):
        drift = 0.004 * math.sin(t / 30)        # cyclical up/down regimes
        px *= (1 + drift + random.uniform(-0.012, 0.012))
        closes.append(round(px, 2))
    highs = [c * 1.012 for c in closes]
    lows = [c * 0.988 for c in closes]
    # occasional volume spikes so the vol_ratio filter can pass
    vols = [1000 * (3.0 if t % 11 == 0 else random.uniform(0.6, 1.2)) for t in range(len(closes))]
    if _sma is None:
        print("technical.py not importable — run from backend dir for the real test."); return
    # relaxed params for the self-test ONLY — just to exercise entries/exits/metrics
    p = Params(rsi_min=40, rsi_max=80, vol_ratio_min=1.2, pct52_min=-40, pct52_max=0)
    trades = simulate_ticker("SYNTH", closes, highs, lows, vols, p)
    print(f"Self-test: generated {len(trades)} trades on synthetic data.")
    print_summary("SELF-TEST (synthetic, not real edge)", summarize(trades))
    print("\nEngine OK. Point it at dse.db from the backend dir for the real backtest.")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--db", default="dse.db")
    ap.add_argument("--tickers", default=None)
    ap.add_argument("--sweep", default=None, choices=["rsi"])
    ap.add_argument("--selftest", action="store_true")
    a = ap.parse_args()
    tickers = a.tickers.split(",") if a.tickers else None

    if a.selftest:
        selftest()
    elif a.sweep == "rsi":
        sweep_rsi(a.db, tickers)
    else:
        trades = run(a.db, tickers)
        print_summary("FULL BACKTEST (default params)", summarize(trades))
        # exit-reason + per-ticker context helps sanity-check
        if trades:
            byt = {}
            for t in trades:
                byt.setdefault(t.ticker, []).append(t.net_ret)
            print("\n  per-ticker expectancy (top/bottom):")
            ranked = sorted(byt.items(), key=lambda kv: statistics.mean(kv[1]))
            for tk, rs in ranked[:3] + ranked[-3:]:
                print(f"    {tk:11} n={len(rs):3d}  exp {statistics.mean(rs)*100:+.2f}%")

      <button class="btn btn-primary" style="margin-top:8px" onclick="navigator.clipboard.writeText(document.getElementById('scan-claude-text').value).then(()=>alert('Copied!'))">Copy to Clipboard</button>
    </div>
  </div>

      <div class="filter-group"><span class="filter-label">Min Tech Score</span><input id="f-techscore" placeholder="0–100" style="width:110px"></div>
